# Completion refactor evidence

This is an implementation journal for
[the completion plan](single-capability-completion-plan.md), not a completion
claim. The full six-capability objective remains active. The current reviewable
architecture, ownership/deletion map and remaining priorities are summarized in
[the framework delivery](single-capability-framework-delivery.md).


## Codex-first native diagnostics (user steering, 2026-10-16)

The user defers Cursor, Claude Code, DeepSeek/API and other native matrices.
Their missing environments no longer block the current Codex milestone. Common
provider/sandbox mechanisms and all existing boundaries remain unchanged; the
later matrices remain explicit follow-up work.

Two separately frozen, single-dispatch diagnostics now pass through the installed
canonical owner, existing selected account, actual Codex 0.162.1 and SSH Docker:
plain text returns exactly `OK`, and a small object schema returns `{"ok": true}`.
Both retain typed native exchange results and reported usage. Requested identity
is gpt-6-sol/high; served model and monetary cost remain unknown. Evidence:
`codex-first-evidence-20261016/evidence.json`. No product changes or vendor loop
were introduced. These show basic native and structured execution, not review or
model quality. The complete review's max-output failure still needs localization;
the initial service-unavailability hypothesis is not established.

The original v8 manifest and all three stage/account states remain unchanged.
Discovery's reservation is still pending; these independent diagnostics neither
replace that case nor authorize its redispatch. Historical blocked audit below
keeps its observed scope; current validation order is Codex first.

## Full-goal blocked audit (2026-10-16)

The same native-quality blocker persists across three consecutive goal turns.
The original Discovery container is confirmed exited (137, not running, not OOM),
but a fresh original-bound result-only read still refuses without a valid worker
terminal receipt. Stage 2 remains `needs_reconciliation` and the native reservation
remains `reserved`. There is no live evaluation controller to wait for. Original
Direct terminal max-output failures also remain unresolved. No model is rerun,
original outcome reclassified or frozen producer edited.

The independently available artifact work is complete: sealed review candidate,
exact-pair deterministic gates, offline hash-required installation/public SDK
proof and scoped production survey. Full quality, remaining actual backend/account
acceptance, complete provider/publication retention and authorized immutable
release/rollout remain open. Further native dispatch needs an explicit disposition
for the original unknown call and usable frozen evaluation profiles/environments;
it cannot bypass the original reservation with a new ID. The full goal is not
complete or narrowed. Audit: `native-quality-all-ten-20261016/blocked-audit-20261016.json`.

## Concrete native comparison and first actual outcomes (2026-10-16)

`native-quality-all-ten-20261016/manifest-v8.json` binds twenty original/current
stages and forty-eight full-workflow/supplement phases, exact installed package
files, source/knowledge/label identities, real native CLI hashes, native roles,
private history retention and the paired 206-native-invocation ceiling. A new
`quality_stage_arm` supervises existing entry points through the neutral process
owner. It has no vendor invocation or retry implementation, preflights every
phase before starting, keeps temporary outputs/traces inside each phase and
refuses implicit relaunch of a partial stage. Source-authored supplements are
separate observations from measured native generation.

Discovery's existing supplement now supports exact committed source and frozen
candidate inputs through the installed Git/index/candidate validators. It no
longer needs an archived excerpt with a synthetic identity for these new cases.
Original SDK Direct preparation binds the declared repository in its runner;
canonical mixed-profile hash lookup is reused by CLI dispatch, native preparation
and subscription replay. Old missing-model callers receive their frozen default
only when explicitly allowed. Source/installed checks pass; runtime/test deltas
are recorded at `quality-stage-composition-20261016/evidence.json`. Product delta
is zero and P736/B939 wheels remain unchanged.

Concrete preparation exposed and preserved several unadmitted failures: bundle
objects existed without a checked-out HEAD; original Strict uses its canonical
PR worktree cache; preselected origins require a parseable forge identity and
still fetch. Final inputs explicitly checkout their frozen pins and the standard
Strict request/worker flow is retained, with each arm's exact canonical worktree
path in the frozen scope. All twelve actual original worker CLI/bridge packets
captured at the fake boundary validate before any native inference. New control
Python 3.12.13 is private and stable; existing RC/retained environments are not
modified. The immutable worker remains Python 3.12.15. Real binary/account controls
and fixed-authority verified TLS checks pass with zero inference.

The first admitted v7 controller attempt failed on a remaining old hash field
before `execute_native_process`; its directory contains only identity control
records. Its original reservation and frozen environment are retained with a
separate zero-dispatch proof. Final v8 adds the canonical shared lookup fix. The
first long-lived proxy replacement also refused an old closed Unix socket; that
known dead socket was replaced before subsequent native execution. Proxy lifetime
now covers the maximum declared stage schedule and it remains privately scoped.

Actual v8 Direct calls from both original and current packages reach native
inference but fail with `max_output_tokens` incomplete responses. Both terminal
failures are explicitly reconciled through the original coordinator, with rerun
forbidden. Tokens/cost/served model were unreported and remain unknown. These are
failed Direct observations, not review completion or quality parity. The same frozen controller subsequently stopped at baseline Discovery. Its original
remote container result records cancellation (-9), but lacks a valid final worker
receipt. An original-bound result-only query is refused by the canonical decoder;
the model outcome, usage and cost remain unknown. Stage 2 needs reconciliation and
its native reservation stays pending. No stage is redispatched or marked passed.
Evidence is `native-quality-all-ten-20261016/discovery-pending-observation-v8.json`;
the current checkpoint is `native-quality-all-ten-20261016/execution-checkpoint-v8.json`. No old paid/pending
pilot, deployment pin or publication was modified. Full native quality, remaining
backend/account/recovery and immutable release/retention/rollout acceptance remain
open; the complete original goal is active.

## Real native accounts and encrypted ZCode cache (2026-10-16)

Real Codex 0.162.1 and ZCode 0.16.9 binaries now run through the shared Docker
backend and local/SSH execution locations. Their original executable hashes are
bound into a fresh image; no source login file enters the image. Initial S25
controls admitted Codex but refused ZCode before any model dispatch. The real
ZCode cache stores selected identity/key values as `enc:v1`, while the previous
projector handled only the plaintext compatibility format.

The provider's existing projector now decodes only the selected identity and its
matching API key using the installed native version's AES-256-GCM protocol. It
uses the native secret override or the trusted cache owner's home/name fallback,
including when a root service reads a UID-1000 cache. It never decrypts/projects
unrelated accounts or refresh state. Plaintext snapshots and serialized worker
contracts stay compatible. Wrong keys, damaged tags/encoding and unsupported
versions refuse with static diagnostics. The original cache remains unchanged.
Encrypted input needs cryptography, included by the existing `kb` extra; plaintext
input still works without that dependency.

Fresh source/installed checks and real local/SSH account controls pass. Both real
CLI versions/identities and projected account kinds agree across locations, and
all controls record zero model calls. Native-cache classification does not prove
API/server entitlement, invoice cost or model quality. Model network/proxy and
independently frozen quality inputs remain required before real model dispatch.

## Current production history payload policy (2026-10-15)

## Implemented migrations

| Slice | Implementation and deleted bodies | Current evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1: credential isolation | Copilot `execution_environment.readonly_environment`, lazily exported through SDK. JSON sessions import it; the bot's `process.review_agent_environment` delegates. Removed the bot's credential tables/filter and the JSON session's own filter body. Subscription allowlisting remains separate. | Nonmutation, repeat application, ambient filtering, model-auth retention, existing bot credential tests and SDK boundary tests pass. |
| 2: process supervision | Copilot `providers.process.run_process` owns process groups, deadlines, cancellation, cleanup and outcomes. `base.run_cli` / `stream_cli` project compatible results; ZCode selects anonymous file spooling and decodes events. Removed old streaming supervision and ZCode timer/kill/drain lifecycle. Explicitly injected legacy buffered runners remain a compatibility seam; production uses the supervisor. | `provider-slices-1-2.xml`: 101 passed, including real fake-executable cleanup, timeout, cancellation, spool losslessness, journal interruption and adapter/archive checks. Additional ownership guard passes. |
| 3: SDK-host communication | ReviewBot `host_client.HostClient` owns spawn, serialization, bounded writes/reads, decoding and close. Direct and Strict translate typed errors; Strict binds its capability handshake to the host generation. Removed both exchange implementations and Strict's automatic mid-call replay. Unknown responses remain pending. | Focused host/recovery/Strict suites: 107 passed. `bot-slices-1-3.xml`: 201 passed in the paired editable environment, including operational capacity, isolated-host end-to-end reviews, credential and boundary tests. Additional host/credential ownership guard passes. |
| 4: diff representation | `review.diff.parse_diff` owns paths, file metadata, hunks, old/new line coordinates, truncation and exact excerpts. Planning, `diff_index`, refinement excerpts and changed-symbol extraction use projections. Removed the planner's header/unquote parser and refinement's raw split/header extraction. | `diff-slice-4.xml`: 198 passed. Added shared-path/anchor tests for quoted UTF-8, renames, binary/deleted/mode-only files, header-shaped content, missing/excess lines and whole-hunk uncertainty; a subsequent plain unified multi-file case also passes. |
| 5: review predicates | `review.dispositions` owns disposition decoding/semantics with coded errors; bot publication supplies safe identifier normalization and frozen references. Direct decoding no longer repeats uniqueness/count predicates. `review.publication_policy.review_evidence_error` composes mode-specific evidence checks; standalone publication now rechecks the canonical withheld-candidate proof. | `publication-slice-5.xml`: 73 passed; `bot-publication-slice-5.xml`: 182 passed. SDK/Direct runtime focused integration also passes. Full completion-policy/accounting audit remains an integration gate. |

Process supervision retains different pipe/spool I/O readers, not different
process lifecycles. The spool reader preserves stdout-before-stderr sweeps and
drains bytes after a producer exits without treating a slow journal as a producer
timeout. DeepSeek's external harness deadline/step-limit policy is not a vendor
CLI subprocess implementation and has not been replaced by this supervisor.

Host tests exercise blocked reads and writes, concurrent request/response pairing,
host-generation changes, malformed replies and the Strict pending-error mapping.
The host client terminates only its own host; durable worker ownership remains
with Copilot. Missing initial distribution metadata caused four integration test
failures under the system Python; those checks are rerun in the editable paired
environment rather than weakening their metadata requirements.

## Knowledge runner migration in progress

`kb_service.quality_review` now owns common judgment dispatch, fixed init-judge
accounting, native receipt construction, input/policy identity receipts, bounded
concurrent scheduling and checkpoint-before-dispatch/result-after-call sequencing.
Block screening/foundation, consistency, facet depth, feature discovery and
PR-history aggregate judgment use its call path. Their rubrics and interpretation
remain explicit stage policies. Discovery native-receipt construction delegates
to the canonical implementation.

Foundation job scheduling and discovery extraction/review/repair scheduling use
`drain_review_tasks`. Domain callbacks translate and save results before the
scheduler admits follow-ups. Budget stops drain in-flight completions. Depth
judgment and PR-history aggregate review use `checkpoint_review`, preserving
their existing keys and adding a history aggregate checkpoint. Native completion
errors now carry dispatch/completion facts so checkpoint recovery distinguishes
known unavailability from an unknown paid call.

Current evidence (overlapping suites, not additive totals):

- `knowledge-runner-core.xml`: 138 passed, including discovery concurrency,
  correction, budget-stop draining, foundation checkpoint feedback and the
  shared runner tests.
- `knowledge-depth-runner.xml`: 170 passed.
- `knowledge-stage-runner.xml`: 148 passed.
- History tests initially exposed over-conservative handling of known failure
  and completed prior-head results. The checkpoint helper was corrected; the
  history/native-archive regression run subsequently passed (`knowledge-history-runner.xml`).

An old Codex command-construction test patched `subprocess.run`; after the

Still audit and consolidate the remaining per-stage correction progression and

## Regression closure pass — 2026-10-12

The first complete bot run reported 20 failures / 2670 passes. Most failures
patched removed private process/exchange seams. Tests now inject buffered model
I/O at the shared JSON-session runner without intercepting unrelated Git calls;
Strict capability and containment tests exercise the shared host client. The
focused five-file bot suite passed all 182 tests (`bot-regressions-2.log`).

Installed-wheel Direct execution exposed a real supervisor regression: a child
that exits without consuming stdin could lose its output/category to EPIPE.
The supervisor now preserves the child's exit/output and retains its watchdog
when the child closes stdin but continues running. Real subprocess tests cover
both cases. The focused Direct suite passed; the obsolete broken-pipe assertion
was replaced by a live-child deadline check.

The quality integration pass found and fixed two additional issues: the governed
improvement judge adapter now returns the canonical ModelReply shape with unknown
native usage explicitly retained; checkpoint interruption no longer erases a
completed receipt from the in-memory record before an outer final save. The
fallback concurrency fixture now models a completed unusable response; an
unknown dispatched failure is intentionally not eligible for fallback.
`copilot-regressions-2.xml` records 95 tests, zero failures/errors/skips.

failures were reproduced as an environment conflict: the system-site-packages
editable environment imports MCP 2.x, while pyproject already requires <2.
A clean source-validation environment is being installed with declared extras
(session 83407); use it for the final full source run after confirming dependencies.
Do not reinterpret these failures as passing tests or change the supported MCP
API to accommodate the contaminated test environment.

needs an end-to-end audit to ensure uncertain receipts cannot become new calls.

Cursor probes now use the same sanitized context as execution. The previous
subprocess invocation bodies were deleted. `shared-auth-probes.xml`: 50 passed.

and the actual public review/session/curation/environment/publication/descriptor
operations, not merely package/API version strings. Offline bundle fixtures were

latest full bundle test run is active (37658), and the installed-wheel verifier

18 initial calls plus bounded repairs, timeout/call/spend ceilings,

and freezing representative input bytes, executing the comparison and recording

performed in this pass.

## Full-suite recovery contract follow-up — 2026-10-12

Collected the existing handles without restarting them: the clean Copilot full
suite finished with 4,470 passed, 5 failed and 20 skipped; the latest release
bundle suite finished successfully (8 tests). Four Copilot failures asserted
automatic replay after an interrupted depth call. Those expectations conflict
with the planned unknown-outcome invariant. Their replacements require retention
of completed drafts and previously accepted facets, unchanged attempt counts,
pending dispatch markers, and no redispatch even on explicit retry. The fifth
failure was a foundation budget-test fixture missing the offered-evidence field
now included in durable dispatch identity; the fixture now supplies it.

The checkpoint error message now directs callers to reconcile the original
dispatch, rather than suggesting a correction could bypass an unresolved call.
The first focused rerun found one remaining legacy expectation: without any
accepted judgment, the resumed stage is empty, not a publishable dry run. That
assertion was corrected. The follow-up suite (handle 17110) completed with
**66 passed**, recorded in `recovery-contracts-final.log/.xml`.

The provider boundary guard now covers every provider module, including auth
probes. It exposed a separately owned external-SDK lifecycle: DeepSeek launches
its bundled runtime through `deepseek_harness` and closes that SDK with a local
watchdog. The SDK is absent from the isolated test environment. This is an open
acceptance item, not evidence of full lifecycle consolidation. Inspect its
deadline/cleanup contract and the current behavior that only traces an
unsupported MCP bridge after execution; the plan requires unsupported requested
capabilities to fail before dispatch. The guard explicitly permits this SDK
watchdog pending that audit while prohibiting independent subprocess launch
bodies in all adapters.

## Requested capability preflight — 2026-10-12

All five native session adapters now call the same
`HarnessTransport.validate_session` check. A requested MCP bridge without the
registry capability fails before adapter setup or SDK construction. The harness
application boundary also checks before marking its accounting facts as sent.
DeepSeek's previous post-execution capability-gap branch was removed; explicit
sessions using native tools remain supported. Updated documentation no longer
claims its generated composition can mount an unavailable bridge.

`provider-preflight.xml`: **115 passed**, covering DeepSeek, Codex, Cursor,
Claude, ZCode, completion and accounting. An additional application-level test
passed independently: unsupported DeepSeek bridge requests construct no SDK and
settle once with `sent=False`. A new full source run writes
`copilot-preflight-full.log/.xml`; its result is pending. The external DeepSeek
SDK's process cleanup/deadline contract remains an open audit item, as do the
other final integration, shadow and release gates already recorded above.

## DeepSeek external SDK inspection — 2026-10-12

Downloaded but did not install or execute `deepseek_harness_sdk-0.1.5rc1`.
Artifact SHA-256 is
`8d9897e97e8a95fd738cb2c3e2c563951d824e43847ec6f3b0fb172c0729b4d8`.
`deepseek-sdk-audit/inspection.json` records extracted-source hashes and findings:
the client merges its config environment into the parent's environment, creates
no process group, and closes only its direct child. Its current config also lacks
the adapter's `cordis` and `session_root` fields. Therefore fake-SDK adapter tests
do not prove compatibility or credential isolation against this artifact.

The adapter's session watchdog starts after SDK startup, and tool-less completion
has no outer absolute deadline. Closing these gaps requires running the SDK under
the canonical process supervisor with a filtered parent environment, preserving
the supported SDK protocol/configuration rather than copying its lifecycle or
temporarily mutating the application's global environment. Version compatibility
must be established explicitly before executing a real request.

Removed the tool-less adapter's broad exception-to-empty-reply conversion. Lost
SDK responses now propagate to `complete_native`, which records sent-but-unknown
completion and unknown usage. A new integration test verifies one settlement and
the original failure facts. Targeted provider/completion/fallback results are in
`deepseek-unknown.xml`. Full suite 61196 remains running against the preceding
source state; it must be collected, not restarted on an observation timeout.

## DeepSeek supervisor cutover — 2026-10-12

Both native sessions and tool-less completions now invoke `deepseek_worker`
through `stream_cli` and the canonical process supervisor. The worker starts
under the filtered environment and receives credentials over stdin. It adapts
SDK results and the existing step cap; it owns no process launch, watchdog or
termination loop. The previous adapter Timer and best-effort close helper were
deleted. SDK stdout is separated from the response protocol, and worker errors
return exception types without potentially sensitive exception messages.

Real subprocess tests cover environment inheritance, stdout contamination,
startup/turn/shutdown hangs and descendant cleanup. `deepseek-worker-final.xml`
has 35 passing tests; a subsequently added incompatible-SDK preflight test also
passed. Protocol-mapping tests explicitly execute the worker body with a fake
SDK; they do not claim live model parity. The installed-wheel harness now includes
the real worker-process tests and is running into `installed-wheels-deepseek/`.

Downloaded SDK `0.1.0rc6` accepts the adapter's existing composition/session
configuration; constructor compatibility was checked without starting a runtime.
The optional `deepseek` extra pins that SDK (which pins its runtime dependency).
Both parent and worker check for the required configuration fields; incompatible
SDKs fail before runtime construction rather than silently dropping sandbox
composition. A paid end-to-end SDK/model run is still unverified. Full suite
61196 predates this cutover and remains an intermediate validation run.

## DeepSeek native notification and partial accounting — 2026-10-12

Inspection of the pinned SDK's `Notification` and `Session.run` revealed that
notifications are slots dataclasses carrying `method/payload`, with native events
inside `payload.event`. The former attribute/`__dict__` handler missed the step
cap for this real shape. The worker now decodes that envelope, and the runaway
test covers both raw events and the SDK envelope.

The worker now streams versioned native event frames before its terminal result.
The parent forwards those frames through the existing native archive hook; its
snapshot retains reported usage/model identity if a later deadline prevents the
final result. Cost reports are preserved, while missing cost or token counts are
not synthesized. SDK logs stay off the protocol channel. A real child-process
test reports usage then hangs and verifies the parent receives the report before
termination; a cost-only report does not invent token counts.

`deepseek-events.xml`: **66 passed**, followed by the cost-only/partial-deadline
test passing after the last accounting adjustment. `deepseek-notifications.xml`
has 28 passing overlapping tests. The full source handle 61196 and installed
wheel handle 18551 were both confirmed live. Their source/build snapshots predate
these notification changes and are intermediate evidence, not final validation.

`complete_native` now collects supported native events independently of archive

`accounting-no-archive.xml`: **64 passed**.

The function-body scan examined 4,852 functions across both production packages
and found no exact duplicate body of at least eight statements after excluding

## Deployment drain includes durable Direct and unresolved outcomes

The rollout-source audit found a remaining production gap in ReviewBot's
`deploy/server-deploy.sh`: it enumerated only `strict_run_id`, and unknown or
interrupted local provider outcomes did not prevent the final stop barrier.
The drain now enumerates provider identities from routed attempts and workflow
jobs, including terminal host rows whose provider may still be running. Saved
local execution bindings select the original run directory through the public
SDK. Bound Strict aliases follow the same path; legacy Strict rows remain
compatible. Missing reservation outcomes, missing bindings, conflicting aliases,
and nonterminal/unknown provider states cannot authorize service retirement.
Remote bindings must match the worker URL and original release controlled by the
deployment transaction. Worker activity does not bypass local bound-run checks.

Focused drain/maintenance tests and the broader deployment/release/worker suites
pass; terminal JUnit evidence is `deploy-drain-focused.xml` and
`deploy-drain-broader.xml` in the completion output root. Shell syntax and diff
checks pass. These are isolated deployment fixtures, not a live inventory or
deployment. No service was switched. The deployment script is outside the frozen
wheels, so package evidence remains valid; release-bundle source identity and

## Agent execution preflights its required repair client

The governed agent-step runner now checks the output-repair client's completion
capability before dispatching its initial investigation. `HarnessLLM` delegates
that check to the existing transport preflight, so capability authority remains
with the adapter. The check intentionally uses the run's client: output repair
uses that client even when an API or native mixture member performs the initial
investigation. Unsupported completion returns a blocked step before evidence
construction, dispatch tracing or paid model execution. Existing API clients
without native completion restrictions retain their behavior.

## Claude terminal-result validation

The shared native invocation previously discarded process exit status and could
turn missing or malformed terminal output into an empty successful completion.

nonzero exit cannot report success. Diagnostics do not copy private stderr into
the exception. Timeout salvage remains unchanged, and valid native error envelopes
remain available for caller-specific interpretation such as budget termination.

## Native usage normalization consolidated further

Claude and Cursor now use `SessionUsage.add_token_usage` instead of their own
integer/default-zero bodies. Missing or malformed counters remain absent from
billing facts; legacy display counters stay numeric. Claude's session and
completion results carry the same reported cache counters and cost. Native cost
validation is shared through `SessionUsage.set_reported_cost`, including explicit
zero and rejection of negative, boolean, nonfinite or string values.

ZCode's separate recovery decoder was removed: `native_snapshot` delegates to
its normal `_usage` implementation. Its result reports are cumulative snapshots,
so the newest report replaces the previous one rather than summing them. Both
session and completion results retain reported cache/cost facts, and absent or
malformed counters are not filled with zero.

**124 tests passed** (`shared-native-usage-final-tests.xml`) across usage,
Claude/Cursor/ZCode transports, shared completion and domain budgeting. The two
new ZCode tests initially had a class-name import typo; the corrected tests and

is not full accounting-workstream closure. Current wheels and full-integration

## DeepSeek native usage decoder consolidated

DeepSeek session, completion and recovery now share `_activity` for native usage
facts. It delegates token and cost validation to `SessionUsage`, replacing its
independent integer/default-zero totals and recovery field-presence decoder.
The pinned native runtime emits a usage chunk and an assistant-message receipt
for each (turn, step); the decoder prefers the message without double counting,
retains the chunk when the worker stops first, and does not add legacy aggregate
reports to native per-step reports. Unbound native reports and missing/malformed
step counters cannot establish complete totals. Served model identity is read
from the native message source as well as legacy envelopes.

**161 tests passed** (`all-native-usage-tests.xml`), followed by **134 consumer and
boundary tests** (`native-usage-consumers-final.xml`). Offline replay of the
previous native fake-model receipts confirms one-call totals 1/1 and two-call
totals 2/2, preservation of pre-message partial usage, and served model identity.
`deepseek-native-usage-replay.json` binds the original receipts and current decoder;
no model or runtime was dispatched by replay. Shared cost normalization also
handles unrepresentably large native amounts as unknown rather than raising.

The remaining caller audit found a structured-session accounting bypass:
`providers/json_session.py` still copies the latest raw event's usage into the
budget settlement. It must delegate to canonical adapter aggregation, including
partial/error paths, while checking compatibility of its public metadata shape.
Consequently this is not full accounting-workstream closure. Current paired
artifacts and full-source evidence still predate the latest changes.

## Agent-session failure receipts share adapter recovery

Added an optional native receipt observer to `AgentSessionRequest` and composed
it in `run_harness_session` on a copy, preserving the caller's request. Codex,
Cursor, Claude, DeepSeek and ZCode now forward native events before downstream
trace, model-identity or result processing can fail. If no outcome usage is
available, the shared boundary delegates recovery to the adapter's existing
snapshot decoder and settles once. Refused outcomes settle as failed rather than
completed, retaining any reported partial usage.

The common model-call trace helper now accepts reported usage and served model
independently of a reply. Both session and completion failure paths populate
the standard trace usage field while retaining empty outputs for absent replies.
This removes the discrepancy between a known budget charge and an empty trace
usage record; it does not fabricate successful output or unknown billing facts.

The initial existing adapter/rebase suite passed **156 tests**. New failure
fixtures first exposed an incorrectly nested DeepSeek fixture usage field; the
fixture was corrected to the native event schema. A broader run initially named
a nonexistent test file and was corrected before executing. The final combined
suite passed **347 tests**, with no failures, errors or skips: all adapter
suites, session/completion usage, budgeting, agent runtime/ensemble/MoA, rebase,
trace storage and review replay capture. `agent-session-receipts-evidence.json`
binds source hashes and reports, including the intermediate 303-test pass.

These are offline source checks. Hard-kill receipt durability, unknown-outcome
reconciliation, current paired wheels/full suites, native policy enforcement,
Codex completion compatibility, quality comparison and rollout still need their

## Unknown idempotency state no longer authorizes another execution

The recovery audit found a concrete bypass of the no-duplicate-dispatch
invariant: unreadable index entries were treated as absent, missing run/status
state could create another run, and retention deliberately deleted bindings
whose run directory was gone. Fixed the shared reader, reservation service and
status claim rules. Invalid/unreadable bindings now require reconciliation;
missing run/status state retains the original ID without a new dispatch.
Rearming requires an explicit null child marker and matching persisted request.
Legacy entries without a fingerprint use the original request to validate
identity. The reaper rereads under the key lock, keeps unresolved/interrupted
bindings regardless of age, and preserves the existing resolved-terminal TTL.
The old test expecting a fresh run after directory loss was changed to enforce
the plan's explicit unknown-outcome invariant.

Validation passed **123 Copilot integration tests**, **74 ReviewBot recovery and
host tests**, and **50 idempotency tests**, all with no failures/errors/skips.
The last set includes two subprocess cases that kill an execution after its
claim and retry the key, once with its status retained and once removed. They
verify no second dispatch or run directory. This is a real process-termination
fixture with simulated work, not a native paid-call or publication crash test.
`idempotency-unknown-recovery-evidence.json` binds source and results. Against
candidate-5, the three production files changed by +38 lines/+26 AST statements.
Current wheels and full paired results predate this fix.

Codex compatibility remains open. Further source inspection confirms that legacy
managed configuration is appended after runtime flags, so the evaluation-only
prototype cannot yet claim a preventive tool boundary across those layers. A
safe namespace-based fixture was attempted, but `unshare` failed and the host's
`user.max_user_namespaces` is 0. `managed-config-probe-admission.json` records the
source hashes and environment result. No host configuration/sysctl was changed,
and no native or paid model call was made for that probe. The original pilot
remains untouched; a different test environment or supported native contract is
still needed for that policy evidence.
# Host ambiguity and unresolved provider states — 2026-10-13

The shared ReviewBot host client rejects successful envelopes without a value,
invalid run identities and non-object poll replies. Only explicit null is a
lookup miss. Real subprocess tests confirm ambiguous lookups do not issue a
replacement `direct_start` and keep the original business key.

A downstream audit found Direct classified `interrupted` as backend failure and
Strict converted unknown states to failed outcomes. Both now call one
`require_resolved_review` predicate before consuming a result. Unresolved states
raise `ProviderReviewPending`, including payloads carrying stale failure fields.
The Strict coordinator handles that signal during conversion as well as polling.
Known terminal failures retain existing fallback policy.

Validation: `host-response-conformance-tests.xml` has 124 passes;
`host-ambiguous-lookup-tests.xml` has 54; `unresolved-provider-state-tests.xml`
has 192; `unresolved-provider-ledger-tests.xml` has 36. All have zero failures,
errors and skips. Suites overlap. The final ledger suite adds six local/remote
cases that reopen SQLite, poll twice, retain the original paid attempt and
capacity, and perform no publication or replacement start.

`output/single-capability-completion-20261012/host-unresolved-recovery-evidence.json`
binds source and report hashes. Tests use fake subprocess/model boundaries, with
real transport and persistence. Candidate-5 predates this change; full paired
artifact validation and actual outcome reconciliation remain open.

## ZCode app-server policy lead — 2026-10-13

concrete alternative to oversized prompt attachments: `app-server` receives

## ZCode native generation verification — 2026-10-13

The native `workspace/generateText` probe now succeeds using an isolated personal
provider-registry fixture and loopback fake endpoint. It sends the complete

reported usage. An injected Bash response is returned as tool-call data without

Inherited-configuration testing found a real limitation: a configured MCP process
starts even with no advertised model tools, and a project-level `mcp.enabled=false`
override did not suppress startup. Empty `mcpServers` in session creation does not
override inherited configuration in the inspected native implementation. Also,
app-server account providers rely on host-supplied account state/runtime headers;
native CLI authentication parity is not established. Do not switch production
completion to this path until both boundaries are resolved.

hashes and the positive, hostile and inherited-configuration observations. All
probe processes are terminal; current production and candidate-6 remain unchanged.


## ZCode MCP configuration resolution — 2026-10-13

Follow-up native probes resolve the earlier inherited-MCP override observation.
The native schema accepts `mcp.servers` but ignores `mcp.enabled`; its feature
switch is `features.mcp`. The existing production
`ZCodeTransport._write_oneshot_config` already writes the correct switch.

A probe invoking that actual helper, with user configuration outside the scratch
workspace, completes a 180,010-byte prompt through the native app-server against
a loopback fake provider: one request, no advertised tools, usage 10 input / 2
output, and no inherited MCP marker. A matching positive control changes only
`features.mcp` to true and observes the inherited process marker. A separate
explicit-feature override probe also suppresses startup. All probes are terminal;
no paid calls, production edits or user-configuration changes occurred.

`zcode-appserver-research/native-configuration-resolution.json` under the completion
output root binds all three reports, the native binary and the production helper's
source hash. It supersedes the unresolved-MCP conclusion in the earlier evidence
without rewriting the original reports. The master plan now reflects this result.

Remaining scope: account authentication parity, hooks/plugins/managed startup
controls, protocol deadlines/cancellation and adapter integration. The production
CLI's oversized-prompt Read exception is unchanged. Do not count these probes as
full tool-policy certification or a completed transport migration.


## ZCode inherited hook boundary — 2026-10-13

Offline native CLI probing found a startup boundary not covered by the previous
MCP checks. An inherited user `SessionStart` process executes with the production
completion configuration. Adding project `hooks.enabled=false` does not suppress
it: the native `vat` hook-root resolver enables hooks when any source enables
hooks. Both terminal probes observed the marker and two loopback fake provider
requests; neither probe timed out. They establish hook execution, not successful
model completion or tool-free behavior.

The attempted production setting and its configuration assertion were removed
because native evidence disproved the fix. The provider source hash again matches
the earlier candidate-6 source. The focused 30-test ZCode run passed while testing
the attempted setting, which demonstrates why argument/configuration tests alone
cannot certify native policy; do not treat it as proof of hook suppression.

Native source also shows that `ZCODE_DATA_BASE_DIR` selects the credential file,
while the fallback cipher key incorporates `os.homedir`. Moving the process home
without a verified credential strategy risks breaking existing login. App-server
mode does not initialize the standalone credential source. Do not silently replace
account authentication or copy credentials into model-readable scratch space.

`zcode-appserver-research/native-hook-boundary-evidence.json` binds both reports,
the native binary, restored production source and relevant source excerpts. All
processes are terminal. No paid calls or user configuration changes occurred.
Next work must establish a supported startup boundary with account compatibility;
a disabled project-hook flag is now ruled out by native evidence.

## External-resource cleanup for daemon-owned execution

Real Personal-Agent probes using candidate-10's process supervisor demonstrated
that absolute timeout, cancellation and observer failure stop the Docker client
but leave the container running. All negative-control containers were explicitly
removed. An initial unprivileged-controller/sudo-client probe also stranded a
root-owned client; manual container cleanup released it and the probe terminated
with failure. Subsequent probes run controller and client at the same privilege.

`providers.process.run_process` now accepts an optional bounded external cleanup
callback. Its stopping thread invokes it exactly once, even when an output
observer is blocked. Cleanup errors preserve captured output in
`ProcessCleanupError`; an existing observer error retains the cleanup failure
without losing the original error. The supervisor retains no Docker/domain
imports. Native probes using the hook removed and verified absence of each
container in all three cases. All 26 supervisor and 34 session/worker checks
passed. No models or credentials were used. Reports and source hashes are in
`personal-agent-sandbox/cleanup-slice-evidence.json` under the completion output.

This production/test slice postdates candidate-10; its wheels and full-suite
reports are historical. The hook is exercised by real Docker probes, but provider
container dispatch, durable identity/reconciliation, native authentication and
restricted model connectivity remain incomplete. No compatibility gate is waived.


## Durable container execution identity

Added internal `providers.container_execution.ContainerExecution` over the
canonical process supervisor and persistence primitives. It exclusively records
the original daemon/name/image/input identity before dispatch, retains start
intent, preserves results after removal and refuses replay of existing or corrupt
bindings. Recovery observes or stops the original container; it cannot start a
replacement. Lost create/start/result responses, concurrent callers, changed
daemons, vanished containers and cancellation during creation are covered. A
zero client exit cannot hide a still-running container or mismatched native exit.

All 39 identity/supervisor tests pass. A real Personal-Agent source-module probe
verified normal stdin/results/removal and killed the controller after readiness.
Recovery found the original running container, refused replay without changing
its binding and stopped it. Stopped crash containers/records are retained as
evidence. Final source hashes and reports are in
`personal-agent-sandbox/container-identity-evidence.json`. No model calls or
credentials were used. These are source-module tests, not installed-wheel tests.

The initial runner deliberately has a fixed no-network, digest-pinned profile.
It is exercised by real daemon probes but is not yet selected by a provider
transport. Model connectivity/authentication, evidence mounts, tool policy and
application/profile integration remain open; this does not restore Codex
completion or close final artifact validation.


## Native Codex inside the remote container

Personal-Agent already has Codex 0.162.1 (outside non-interactive SSH PATH) and
a ChatGPT login. An isolated image pins its existing native executable, code-mode
helper, ripgrep and model catalog over a digest-pinned Python base. No credentials
were copied into the image. The initial probe omitted the code-mode helper and
reported an item error despite a zero exit; retained as negative packaging
evidence, not a tool-policy pass. The completed image passes an offline native
call to a loopback fake Responses endpoint inside the no-network container:
expected JSON, one gpt-6-sol request, usage 10 input / 2 output, no native error
events. A PATH-alias warning under temporary CODEX_HOME remains.

A separate no-network login-status check mounted only the existing auth.json
read-only as its owner UID and recognized ChatGPT login; the host file was
unchanged, credentials were neither copied nor printed, and the probe container
was removed. This proves cache recognition, not token refresh or live network
authentication. The mount would be readable by tools sharing that UID; this
probe is not authorization to expose it to model-driven commands. Native network
policy and authentication composition remain open. Official storage/headless
reference: https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/auth .

The container runner now also accepts immutable local sha256 image IDs, needed
for unpushed validation images. All 40 identity/supervisor checks pass. Image,
source and test evidence is bound in
`personal-agent-sandbox/native-container-evidence.json`. No paid calls occurred;
provider completion integration and the two Codex compatibility failures remain
open. These source-module probes do not replace installed-wheel validation.


## Native container ID binding

Four new regressions reproduced a recovery gap in the internal container runner:
name/label matching accepted a replacement container and could remove it; missing
native identity did not prevent cleanup. The runner now records the daemon-issued
64-character container ID immutably before startup and includes it in start/result
receipts. Inspect, start, stop and remove use that ID. Missing/corrupt identity
blocks mutation; name-based observation after a lost create response explicitly
reports unconfirmed identity and cannot dispatch or clean up. Original receipts
remain readable after the original container is removed.

All 44 identity/supervisor tests pass. A real Personal-Agent probe removed the
original container and created another with its name/label: recovery and removal
refused the replacement, original receipts/binding remained unchanged, and the
replacement was untouched until explicit fixture cleanup. Reports/source hashes
are bound in `personal-agent-sandbox/container-native-id-evidence.json`. No paid
calls occurred. Provider connectivity/authentication composition and full paired
artifact validation remain open; this slice does not close those gates.


## Model connectivity without a container network interface

Added canonical `providers.network_relay`: a per-execution Unix CONNECT endpoint
with fixed authority-to-destination mapping, bounded connection count and buffers,
half-close handling, loopback-only container adapter and active-connection shutdown.
It neither terminates TLS nor reads model credentials. Unsupported destinations
and ambiguous requests are rejected before opening an upstream connection.
The first test collection used pytest's reserved `request` parameter; renaming
that fixture parameter corrected collection without product changes.

The durable runner accepts a dedicated read-only model-proxy socket mount while
retaining `--network=none`. It refuses the Docker control socket, missing sockets
and socket replacement during creation, and binds the selected path/device/inode.
All 56 relay/identity/supervisor tests pass. A real Personal-Agent container
reached only the configured fake endpoint, rejected an unlisted destination and
left the target with exactly one connection. Its runtime still reported network
mode none. Evidence: `personal-agent-sandbox/network-container-evidence.json`.

These checks prove destination routing, not native HTTPS/authentication or
provider-side tool restrictions. Credentials and refresh remain native/provider
composition work; CONNECT cannot constrain paths inside encrypted traffic. No
paid calls occurred. Provider selection, installed artifacts and full acceptance
remain open.

## Native HTTPS and shared deadlines through the model relay

The native Codex CLI inside a no-network container now has a verified end-to-end
HTTPS path through the canonical loopback/Unix relay to a fixed fake endpoint.
A fixture CA is trusted only for that run. The trusted case produces one
gpt-6-sol request, JSON {"ok":true} and usage 10 input / 2 output; the existing
Codex decoder parses both without a new interpretation implementation.

The first fixture exceeded the Unix socket path limit and failed before any
request. Shortening fixture names fixed setup. The initial protocol check then
failed its prompt-exit assumption: native Codex waited/reconnected past 35 seconds
for untrusted TLS and denied-authority controls. Diagnostics confirmed unknown-CA
alerts and zero HTTP model requests; those failed reports remain historical.
No native retry behavior was changed or represented as fixed.

A separate supervised mode removes the fixture's inner timeout and exercises the
production container deadline directly. Both negative cases stop their original
containers at about 10.3 seconds for a 10-second deadline, retain partial native
error events and send zero model requests. The trusted case finishes normally.
Evidence, including the initial failures and canonical decoding, is bound in
`personal-agent-sandbox/native-https-evidence.json`. No paid calls or real account
credentials were used. Live account refresh, provider-profile wiring, tool-policy
acceptance and final artifact validation remain open.

### Container worker at the common CLI boundary (2026-10-13)

Added the packaged `infermatrix_copilot.container_worker` entry point and
`ContainerExecution.run_command`. The common `run_cli` boundary can explicitly
receive that executor (bound to an immutable image); legacy injected runners and
host stdin handles cannot be combined with it. Native commands and stdin travel
in a versioned envelope, with no provider-specific command construction, event
decoding or accounting duplicated in the worker. This initial profile accepts
image paths, `/tmp/work`, no host environment, stdin evidence, and the existing
optional proxy. It is not yet selected by provider factories or application
profiles and does not establish tool-free execution or authentication parity.

The first subprocess tests caught newline loss from using the existing line
event callback for forwarding. Added a byte-chunk callback to the canonical
process supervisor, preserving the existing line callback contract. The worker
now forwards exact native bytes, including unterminated output, through that
callback. Both controller and worker use the same supervisor implementation.

Validation: `container-worker-tests.xml` records **115 passed**, zero failures,
errors or skips across worker, container identity, process, network relay, Codex
and completion tests. The real Docker source-module probe on Personal-Agent
records exact Unicode/newline output, stderr and nonzero native exit status;
a separate deadline case stops the original container with partial output.
Both results were durably read back and both containers removed. The probe image
is `sha256:0d967e97c1c492756e0aea9ce51b724612853bbbed7b350175fdbb805156ad4e`.
The stopped image assembly container was also removed. Reports and executable
probe are under `output/single-capability-completion-20261012/personal-agent-sandbox/`.
No paid model calls or real credentials were used. This image contains selected
source modules with minimal package initializers, not an installed wheel;
installed packaging, real authentication, trusted application profile selection,
and the original compatibility failures remain open.

### Explicit contained completion through the Codex owner (2026-10-13)

Added `ContainerCompletionProfile`, selected explicitly through
`transport_for_id(..., completion_profile=...)` and the improvement judge's
`completion_profile` argument. It binds one durable executor, immutable image
and absolute image-native CLI path. Existing/default no-tools completion remains
unsupported; no backend capability has been relabeled. Unsupported provider
profiles, reused attempt bindings, and mixed host runner/container selection
fail before model dispatch. Applications must authorize each new attempt; this
profile does not allocate replacement attempts.

Codex's existing `_run` builds the contained command, uses the common `run_cli`
executor boundary, and retains the same event, text and usage decoders. The
container branch ignores user configuration, starts ephemerally and disables
native web search. The Docker sandbox supplies local containment; it does not
attest absence of remote/provider-side tools. Post-call tool-use rejection stays
in the canonical audit. Native errors, nonzero command exits, missing terminal
results and deadline truncation cannot become successful verdicts even when
partial output contains valid JSON. Partial usage still reaches the shared
completion accounting boundary.

`container-completion-tests.xml` covers the actual judge → registry → Codex →
common CLI → container-envelope path with only the Docker boundary substituted,
plus process/worker/network and existing Codex/completion regressions. It is
not live authentication, native model-quality, or installed-image evidence.
The prior real worker Docker probe remains separately scoped. Default workflow
configuration has not been switched: native authentication/refresh and installed
packaging must be established before that cutover. The original two legacy
Codex judge compatibility failures remain open; their expectations were not
weakened.

### Installed worker image validation (2026-10-13)

Built the current provider wheel (`5b1b471e878556de04ecebb673cc8e827b2006ec8d34561ba04e7349a184e0a6`)
and installed it offline in the native Codex fixture image with all 46 prior
candidate dependency pins. `pip check` passed. No source modules or minimal

`sha256:3b6e549fa58d4c34a8e20a91e5046fb4e16e10eb8cc16d865b5681fc25146ed3`.

The first run through the production worker/profile imported from installed

The initial attempt to extract JUnit after stopping also failed because /tmp is

Native Codex also completed against the existing local fake endpoint through

### Recovery of an exact contained completion (2026-10-13)

Added `ContainerExecution.get_command_result` and the Codex transport's
`get_completion_result`. Recovery verifies the original pinned image, worker
command and full request-envelope digest (native argv plus frozen prompt) before
reading the existing result. Registry construction validates profile shape
without requiring a new attempt; dispatch preflight still rejects every existing
binding. Recovery needs neither a live proxy nor a running container. Missing
results remain pending, and no path automatically starts replacement work.

Normal execution and recovery now share Codex command construction, event
handling, tool audit, terminal/error checks and reply projection. Recovery is
read-only: it deliberately does not acquire or settle domain accounting, leaving
that authority with the original receipt owner. Tests reconstruct a transport
after container removal, verify reply/usage parity, reject changed prompt/model/
image and missing results, and fail if recovery accesses Docker or accounting.

`container-recovery-tests.xml`: **130 passed**, zero failures/errors/skips.
On Personal-Agent, the new source controller recovered the exact saved outcome
of the previously removed installed-worker native fixture, with daemon access
and dispatch explicitly forbidden. A changed prompt was rejected. Evidence:
`personal-agent-sandbox/removed-worker-recovery.json` and its input case.
No model calls occurred. The prior installed wheel predates this recovery change;
its results remain historical packaging evidence, not a claim about this source.
Authentication, application receipt reconciliation/profile selection, original
compatibility failures and full-plan integration/quality/release gates remain.

### Authentication scope and synthetic refresh probes (2026-10-13)

Fixed the contained Codex profile's subscription-billing attestation: a host
login cannot establish that an isolated worker uses the same account. Until a
profile-scoped native authentication probe exists, container completion reports
no confirmed subscription billing. The host path is unchanged. The focused
Codex/profile suite records **24 passed** in `container-auth-scope-tests.xml`.

Read-only metadata inspection on Personal-Agent confirmed native `chatgpt`
authentication, access and refresh credentials present, and mode 0600. No
credential values were printed or copied. Asked whether that login is shared
with other interactive sessions/jobs; this remains unanswered and is required
before choosing ownership of writable native credential state.

Official guidance confirms file-backed native refresh/persistence and requires
one serialized owner per cache:
https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/auth/ci-cd-auth
It does not justify copying a shared session into independently refreshing
workers or replacing native refresh with a new OAuth implementation.

Synthetic-only tests in the installed worker image exposed an unresolved native
fixture issue: a custom provider requiring OpenAI auth completed its fake model
request but did not refresh the stale synthetic cache. Using the default OpenAI
provider instead attempted api.openai.com WebSocket traffic despite the fixture's
chatgpt_base_url; Docker network=none blocked it, no local request arrived, and
the fixture deadline ended the probe. Captured diagnostics and original failed
assertions are preserved; neither result proves authentication/refresh parity.
All probe containers were stopped and removed. No real model calls or real
credential mutations occurred. Evidence: `personal-agent-sandbox/native-refresh-evidence.json`.

The contract matrix now distinguishes the implemented scoped Docker components
from remaining authentication/default-workflow certification. The full refactor
remains active, including integration, compatibility, ownership and release gates.

### Knowledge review unknown-outcome ownership (2026-10-13)

Regression tests reproduced four gaps before the fix: block judgment converted a
lost dispatched response to an ordinary human verdict, and init policy identity
did not include quality_review.py, container_worker.py or execution_environment.py.
Block and consistency review now propagate unresolved native outcomes, matching
depth review. `budgeting.native_outcome_unknown` is the single predicate used by
quality checkpoints/receipts, discovery, foundation capture, depth and generator
fallback; the repeated predicate bodies were removed. Depth's independent
reply-digest fallback now delegates to the canonical native receipt function.
The three missing shared implementation dependencies now affect init fingerprints.

Tracing the standalone caller required a related persistence change: intake now
writes its exact draft and a `review_pending` changeset before gate dispatch,
consuming the input events in the same fenced transaction. The existing
`stage_intake` ledger method finalizes only that expected pending state and
records terminal verdicts atomically. This introduces no second ledger. Lost
intent replies, native responses, settlement and lease ownership retain the
original files and prevent the intake poller from redrafting. The old lease-loss
test intentionally expected an event to become claimable again; it now asserts
the stronger required behavior: original pending intent and draft retained,
without a terminal decision or duplicate paid dispatch. The two original Codex
compatibility tests remain unchanged.

Validation: `quality-unknown-call-final.xml` covers init execution, quality,
foundation/discovery/depth, intake, fallback and shared budgets;
`quality-gate-intent-tests.xml` covers intake/flow/refinement/reviewed events,
maintenance and checkpoint recovery. Exact counts and current production hashes
are in `quality-unknown-call-evidence.json`. These source suites passed; there
were no model calls. The earlier installed worker image predates this slice.

Remaining follow-up identified during caller tracing: sweep, refine, rebuild and
correction can generate work before reaching gate_and_stage. Their admission must
recognize existing review_pending intents before generation, and known results
still need explicit reconciliation. Current intake polling is covered; this slice
does not claim complete recovery closure for all workflow families. Authentication
ownership remains awaiting the existing user question; full-plan acceptance is
still open.

### Pending review admission across legacy workflows (2026-10-13)

Added one `runtime.require_settled_reviews` admission policy over the existing
changeset ledger. Sweep/purge invoke it before sweep progress or generation;
refine/rebuild invoke it before draft work or parent state changes; maintenance
correction/calibration invoke it before creating their budgeted gateway. New
refine/rebuild intents persist their parent ID. Correction scopes to the existing
unit ID. A pending review for another explicitly bound parent/unit does not block
independent work; older intents lacking that binding conservatively require
reconciliation. No path clears a pending intent to obtain permission for a new
model call.

Validation: **132 passed** across admission, refinement, flow, maintenance,
sweep-audit and prose correction. An additional admission/recovery run has
**16 passed**, including real SQLite/filesystem scenarios that lose the child
judge response after normal intake, reconstruct the pending refine/rebuild,
and confirm retry neither calls a model nor mutates the parent. The latter run
adds two scenarios to the overlapping admission suite, not 16 disjoint tests.
The first test invocation named a nonexistent sweep test file and collected no
tests; corrected runs above are the validation evidence.

Source bindings and reports: `quality-review-admission-evidence.json`,
`quality-review-admission-tests.xml`, `quality-review-admission-recovery.xml`.
This closes the identified replacement-work admission gap, not all recovery:
explicit settlement/reconciliation of known pending gate results and interrupted
model generation still need audit. Current installed artifacts predate these
source changes. No paid calls; authentication ownership and full-plan acceptance
remain open.

### Completed knowledge gate receipts (2026-10-13)

The shared `runtime.gate_and_stage` now publishes an immutable private receipt
under `gate-results/<changeset_id>.json` before settling the ledger. It preserves
the original intent detail/generation, pre-dispatch draft hash, full decision and
the exact settlement arguments. Normal ledger settlement uses that same argument
object and stores the receipt hash. Publication uses the existing durable byte
writer, with the new directory also fsynced. No second gate implementation was
introduced. Unknown judge outcomes have no completed receipt and remain pending.

Fault tests cover receipt storage failure, settlement failure before commit and
a lost reply after commit; intake does not issue another model call. The broad
knowledge workflow suite passed **238 tests**. After adding directory fsync and
the committed-reply-loss case, the final focused intake/admission suite passed
**69 tests**. These overlapping reports are not additive; the broad report
predates those final changes. Hashes and scope are recorded in
`quality-gate-result-evidence.json` alongside both XML reports.

This preserves completed paid work; explicit receipt reconciliation remains
unfinished. It must restore sweep and maintenance continuation state before
clearing their admission blockers, and preserve companion-work behavior. No
automatic pending-state clearing or paid replay was added. Installed artifacts,
paired integration and the full completion plan remain open.

### Committed gate continuation in sweep and maintenance (2026-10-13)

Reproduced two sweep crash windows (gate return and aggregate release before
progress checkpoint): both issued three extra model calls on retry. Sweep now
persists a unique instance before work and binds every gate intent to that
instance, release range and page/T1/purge unit. Live staging and restoration use
one progress projection. Resume restores completed units and held changesets
from committed decisions before generation, retaining aggregate breaker inputs.
Pending gates remain blocked; legacy rows without exact bindings are not guessed.

Reproduced duplicate changesets after a lost gate return for both correction and
maintenance calibration. Both now reuse their original committed changeset for
an identical run/unit request, then call the same continuation as normal staging.
The request binds the unit, source finding, base snapshot/content, generation and
gate policies, release/date and phase. Changed or missing bindings require
reconciliation before model work. This does not clear unknown gate intents.

Validation: all four regressions failed before their fixes; **133 tests passed**
in the final flow, sweep audit, review admission, maintenance flow/workflow and
prose-correction suite. It includes changed-request rejection without model calls.
Reports overlap with the focused runs. Current source hashes and exact reports
are in `gate-continuation-evidence.json`; installed artifacts predate this work.
No paid model calls occurred.

Still open: settling a pending gate from its immutable completed receipt;
companion continuation; unknown/interrupted generation before gate reservation;
legacy sweep rows without reconstructable unit identity; full paired integration,
native authentication and quality/rollout acceptance. These changes establish
continuation prerequisites, not completion of the full recovery contract.

### Shared gate receipt settlement and operator recovery (2026-10-13)

Normal gate execution and `kb reconcile-review CHANGESET_ID` now use the same
`gate_receipts.settle_gate_result` implementation. The command opens only the
ledger and existing artifacts: no registry synchronization, runtime construction,
model calls or publication. It holds the existing single-writer lease. Receipt
identity, original intent, draft digest and settlement binding are checked;
repository generation and original intent are checked again inside the fenced
ledger transaction. A matching committed receipt is idempotent, including a lost
commit reply, without inserting another verdict/human-queue entry.

Receipts now explicitly record whether a companion continuation is required.
The live path retains that continuation. Operator recovery refuses those pending
results until companion recovery is implemented; it does not clear their blocker.
Older sweep/correction receipts lacking their exact continuation binding also
remain pending. Missing, malformed, changed-draft/intent and obsolete-generation
results cannot authorize replacement model work. Maintenance policy fingerprints
include the new settlement owner.

Validation: **194 passed** in the broad intake, admission, flow, refinement,
maintenance and new receipt suite. The final **18-test** recovery selection adds
pending-receipt settlement followed by sweep and correction/calibration resume,
with no extra model calls or changesets. It also covers CLI retry, missing and
invalid inputs, lost commit reply and a generation change during settlement.
The reports overlap; exact scopes, hashes and counts are recorded in
`gate-settlement-evidence.json`. No paid calls or installed-wheel claims.

Remaining recovery work includes companion continuation and unknown generation
before gate intent creation. Legacy ambiguous bindings still require explicit
reconciliation. Full paired integration, authentication, quality and rollout
acceptance remain open; the overall goal is not complete.

### Atomic companion continuation from completed gate receipts (2026-10-13)

Removed the independent `stage_companion` write sequence. The canonical pure
companion planner now freezes its edits, identity, original base and reference
bindings in the gate receipt before settlement. The shared receipt path stores
that artifact immutably; the existing fenced ledger transaction creates the
companion and human-queue entry together with the parent decision and waiting
link. A failed child insert rolls back the parent update and verdicts. Normal
execution and operator recovery use this same path, without a companion bypass.
Publication remains in `publish_companion`; the recovery command only stages.
A prepared null companion preserves the existing no-automatic-rewrite outcome.
Legacy receipts that require a companion but lack prepared edits remain pending.

Validation: **193 passed** in companion, receipt, intake, flow, refinement and
maintenance suites. Fault injection covers artifact failure, SQL failure inside
the parent/child transaction and a lost commit reply. Recovery twice yields one
linked companion, no extra model call and no publication; subsequent host
publication remains idempotent. The former crash-before-link test now explicitly
constructs the old persisted unlinked shape (the new atomic transaction cannot
create it) and still verifies rebuilding after companion merge.

Added ledger and companion implementation dependencies to maintenance policy
fingerprinting. The subsequent **76-test** API/maintenance-flow suite passes,
including invalidation when receipt, ledger or companion code changes. Reports
overlap; hashes and exact scope are in `companion-gate-recovery-evidence.json`.
No paid calls, installed-wheel or quality/rollout claims.

Remaining: unknown/interrupted generation before gate intent creation, ambiguous
legacy recovery, final paired integration, native authentication and the frozen
quality/rollout acceptance gates. The full refactor remains active.

### Unknown generator outcomes versus schema repair (2026-10-13)

Three regressions demonstrated that a ModelUnavailable error containing
“failed its schema” triggered all repair attempts despite explicit native
sent=true/completed=false metadata. Shared `intake.draft_operations` now consults
the canonical `native_outcome_unknown` predicate before interpreting repair
wording. Intake, sweep and the direct candidate loop propagate the original
unknown error after one call; known schema failures retain bounded repair.

Intake now records an observed unknown generator result as `draft_unknown` in its
existing fenced event transition, rather than making it pending again. Polling
cannot redraft it. `kb status` exposes these events as `unresolved_draft_events`.
This does not fabricate a completed draft or allow the gate-recovery command to
settle a generator result. Known unavailable calls retain the existing retry
behavior. No new provider or generation loop was introduced.

Validation: **178 passed** after the shared repair fix, **65 passed** after intake
admission changed, and **2 passed** for final status reporting plus no-redraft
behavior. Reports overlap and each covers its stated source stage; exact hashes
and the three original failures are in `draft-unknown-evidence.json`. No paid
calls or installed artifact claims.

This handles an observed unknown result, not a process crash before the error is
recorded. Durable pre-dispatch generation identities/results across intake,
refinement and sweep are still required; other workflow catches also need that
shared checkpoint migration. Full paired integration, native authentication and
quality/rollout gates remain open.

### Refinement generation through shared durable checkpoints (2026-10-13)

Added a typed-draft adapter to the existing `quality_review.checkpoint_review`
executor and migrated refinement generation to it. There is no new dispatch or
repair loop. Its stable unit is the original parent changeset. The ledger cursor
saves intent under the existing lease before drafting; the binding includes base
SHA, repository generation, requested role/fallback and a digest of generation
inputs and prompt. Completed draft operations/files and accepted trace identity
are saved before returning to the gate. Changed completed inputs require
reconciliation instead of silently purchasing another generation.

Ledger cursor writes now optionally use the existing fenced transaction, while
legacy callers retain their prior API. Unknown native outcomes and process
interruption retain the dispatch marker. Resume cannot issue another generation;
a completed draft resumes through the normal full gate and preserves the original
accepted trace key. This receipt is a generated candidate, not a gate approval.

Validation: **103 passed** in final refinement, operation-drafting, admission,
shared quality-runner and flow suites. Added tests simulate interruption during a
native call, an observed unknown native result, and a crash after draft persistence
but before gate reservation. They verify no replacement generation, input/model
binding refusal, unchanged parent on uncertainty, and original trace linkage on
successful resume. Final source/report hashes: `refine-draft-checkpoint-evidence.json`.
No paid calls or installed-wheel/quality acceptance claims.

Still open: migrating intake (including evolved execution) and sweep generation to
the same checkpoint mechanism, full execution-profile/release bindings, recovery
of unresolved native generation, legacy ambiguities and final paired integration.
Authentication and quality/rollout gates remain open; the full goal stays active.

### Sweep generation migrated to the shared draft checkpoint adapter (2026-10-13)

Sweep page generation now uses the same typed-draft checkpoint adapter as
refinement, which delegates dispatch persistence and result reuse to the existing
quality checkpoint runner. The adapter accepts the caller's generation callback
and policy inputs; it does not duplicate a sweep generation or repair loop.
Sweep freezes its own prompt/repair policy, page input, release diff and hints,
base SHA, generation and requested model role. Its business unit binds the sweep
instance, page and existing page-attempt number.

A completed page candidate (including keep/no-op) survives interruption before
gate reservation. The accepted trace key and served generator label survive
reuse. Known exhausted repair attempts are persisted as rejected candidates;
the existing MAX_PAGE_ATTEMPTS policy advances only those known failures to a
new business attempt. An unresolved dispatch never reaches that failure path
and cannot silently buy a replacement call.

Validation: **96 passed** across sweep/flow, refinement, operation drafting,
sweep audit and pending-review admission. New fault tests cover observed unknown
outcomes, interruption during a native call, and completed generation interrupted
before gate reservation. They verify one generator call, retained dispatch state,
and reuse through the first full gate. Existing bounded page-retry and aggregate
breaker tests also pass. Exact source hashes and report are in
`sweep-draft-checkpoint-evidence.json`. No paid calls or installed artifact claims.

Remaining: intake generation (including evolved execution), full trusted
execution-profile/release binding, explicit unresolved-native-generation recovery,
legacy ambiguities, final paired integration and authentication/quality/rollout
acceptance. The overall refactor remains active.

### Intake and evolved generation migrated to shared draft checkpoints (2026-10-13)

Intake now uses the same typed-draft/checkpoint adapter as refinement and sweep,
wrapping the existing ordinary/evolved selection in one generation callback.
Intent is saved before either execution path; complete candidate and accepted
trace link are saved before batch merge, event settlement and gate reservation.
The binding includes the base SHA, evidence/context, generation policy, effective
settings digest and active-registry byte digest. No provider/repair loop was
introduced. The full trusted execution-profile/release verification is still a
separate open requirement; these request hashes are not its certification.

Known batch conflicts explicitly advance a persisted per-event draft attempt,
so the original redraft behavior is preserved. Unavailability preserves that
attempt number; unknown outcomes cannot become a new business attempt.
Evolved execution exceptions retain native completion facts; missing facts at
that boundary are treated as uncertain rather than evidence of no dispatch.

Validation: **166 passed** in final intake, replay capture, flow, refinement,
receipt, operation-drafting and companion suites. New tests cover interruption
during ordinary/evolved execution, evolved response loss, completed candidates
interrupted before gate/event settlement, and known-conflict redrafting. The
first integration run exposed a replay-capture diagnostic ordering regression
for malformed active-registry JSON; hashing the original registry bytes for the
binding preserves the existing capture/validation sequence and the final suite
passes unchanged replay-limit assertions. Evidence: `intake-draft-checkpoint-evidence.json`.
No paid calls, installed-wheel or quality/rollout claims.

Remaining: full execution-location/profile/release binding, explicit reconciliation
of unresolved native generation, ambiguous legacy state, final source and installed
paired integration, native authentication and the frozen quality/rollout gates.
The overall refactor remains active.

Recorded source hashes before running both full suites, then verified no source

### Settle parallel work against its reserved identity (2026-10-13)

A reproduced shared-journal regression showed that settlement recomputed its
identity from the current stage/task binding. If that binding changed while the
worker ran, normal completion could leave the original intent unresolved; a
failed result save could restore a new identity beside the original intent.
The new regression failed in both success and save-failure cases before the fix.

`DispatchJournal.begin` now copies the binding before checkpointing it, isolating
nested request fields from later mutation. `drain_review_tasks` retains the
returned identity/attempt beside each future and passes that exact scope into
settlement. `DispatchJournal.settle` validates and uses the retained binding;
it does not call the stage's identity function again. Failed settlement restores
the original binding. Missing or changed intent refuses the domain settlement
callback and cannot schedule follow-up work. This changes the shared mechanism
used by foundation/discovery; no independent stage loop was added.

Validation: **189 passed** across foundation feedback, parallel/publication and
discovery/boundary/consolidation/shared-runner tests; **38 passed** in the final
shared-runner suite including missing/changed-intent cases. The original two
failing regressions and final reports remain archived with exact source hashes
in `journal-settlement-evidence.json`. All test handles are terminal.

This fixes original-identity settlement inside a live coordinator. It does not
establish full release/profile binding or recover an unknown native result after
a process crash. Candidate 11 wheel results predate this production change.
Native compatibility/authentication, full ownership closure, quality comparison
and rollout still require separate evidence; the overall goal remains active.
